java.util.*;\n30\t\n31\timport static org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.util.NumberHelper.eitherAreNaN;\n32\t\n33\t/**\n34\t * This implementation extends the default one in Gremlin to avoid throwing exception in case the comparison is between different types or just with a null value.\n35\t * Throwing exceptions in Java is expensive and should be avoided for such cases.\n36\t *\n37\t * @author Luca Garulli (l.garulli@arcadedata.com)\n38\t * @author Mike Personick (http://github.com/mikepersonick)\n39\t */\n40\tpublic abstract class GremlinValueComparator implements Comparator<Object> {\n41\t\n42\t  /**\n43\t   * Orderability comparator allows for a total order across all types (no type error exceptions).\n44\t   */\n45\t  public static final GremlinValueComparator ORDERABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n46\t\n47\t    /**\n48\t     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Orderability semantics.\n49\t     */\n50\t    @Override\n51\t    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n52\t      // nulls first\n53\t      if (f == null || s == null)\n54\t        return f == s ? 0 : f == null ? -1 : 1;\n55\t\n56\t      final Type ft = Type.type(f);\n57\t      /*\n58\t       * Do a quick check to catch very common cases - class equality or both numerics. Even if these are false we\n59\t       * could still be dealing with the same type (e.g. different implementation of List), but let the type\n60\t       * lookup method handle that.\n61\t       */\n62\t      final Type st = f.getClass().equals(s.getClass()) || (f instanceof Number && s instanceof Number) ? ft : Type.type(s);\n63\t\n64\t      return ft != st ? ft.priority() - st.priority() : comparator(ft).compare(f, s);\n65\t    }\n66\t\n67\t    /**\n68\t     * Test for equivalence (Orderability semantics).\n69\t     */\n70\t    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n71\t      return compare(f, s) == 0;\n72\t    }\n73\t  };\n74\t\n75\t  /**\n76\t   * Comparability has very similar semantics to orderability with the following exceptions:\n77\t   * <p>\n78\t   * 1. NaN is not equal to anything, including itself, and cannot be compared to anything:\n79\t   * equals(NaN, anything) = FALSE\n80\t   * compare(NaN, anything) = ERROR\n81\t   * 2. Unlike Orderability, Comparability is limited to a single type space:\n82\t   * compare(type1, type2) = ERROR\n83\t   * <p>\n84\t   * Note that because of type errors for Comparability, equals(a,b) does not necessarily produce the same result\n85\t   * as compare(a,b) == 0. Make sure to use equals(a,b) for P.eq/neq.\n86\t   */\n87\t  public static final GremlinValueComparator COMPARABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n88\t\n89\t    /**\n90\t     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Comparability semantics. Throws type errors for NaN comparison\n91\t     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n92\t     *\n93\t     * Use this method for P.lt/lte/gt/gte.\n94\t     */\n95\t    @Override\n96\t    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n97\t      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n98\t      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n99\t        throwTypeError();\n100\t\n101\t      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n102\t      if (!comparable(f, s))\n103\t        throwTypeError();\n104\t\n105\t      // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n106\t      final Type type = Type.type(f);\n107\t      return comparator(type).compare(f, s);\n108\t    }\n109\t\n110\t    /**\n111\t     * Test two Gremlin values for equality per the Comparability semantics. Personick (http://github.com/mikepersonick)\n */\npublic abstract class GremlinValueComparator implements Comparator<Object> {\n\n  /**\n   * Orderability comparator allows for a total order across all types (no type error exceptions).\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator ORDERABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Orderability semantics.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // nulls first\n      if (f == null || s == null)\n        return f == s ? 0 : f == null ? -1 : 1;\n\n      final Type ft = Type.type(f);\n      /*\n       * Do a quick check to catch very common cases - class equality or both numerics. Even if these are false we\n       * could still be dealing with the same type (e.g. different implementation of List), but let the type\n       * lookup method handle that.\n       */\n      final Type st = f.getClass().equals(s.getClass()) || (f instanceof Number && s instanceof Number) ? ft : Type.type(s);\n\n      return ft != st ? ft.priority() - st.priority() : comparator(ft).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test for equivalence (Orderability semantics).\n     */\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      return compare(f, s) == 0;\n    }\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Comparability has very similar semantics to orderability with the following exceptions:\n   * <p>\n   * 1. NaN is not equal to anything, including itself, and cannot be compared to anything:\n   * equals(NaN, anything) = FALSE\n   * compare(NaN, anything) = ERROR\n   * 2. Unlike Orderability, Comparability is limited to a single type space:\n   * compare(type1, type2) = ERROR\n   * <p>\n   * Note that because of type errors for Comparability, equals(a,b) does not necessarily produce the same result\n   * as compare(a,b) == 0. Make sure to use equals(a,b) for P.eq/neq.\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator COMPARABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Comparability semantics. Throws type errors for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.lt/lte/gt/gte.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n      final Type type = Type.type(f);\n      return comparator(type).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test two Gremlin values for equality per the Comparability semantics. Returns false for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.eq/neq.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // shortcut a long, drawn out element by element comparison\n      if (containersOfDifferentSize(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n (GremlinTypeErrorException ex) {\n        /**\n         * By routing through the compare(f, s) path we expose ourselves to type errors, which should be\n         * reduced to false for equality:\n inside of collections.\n         */\n        return false;\n      }\n    }\n\n    private boolean containersOfDifferentSize(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      if (f instanceof Collection collection && s instanceof Collection collection1)\n        if (collection.size() != (collection1.size()))\n          return true;\n\n      if (f instanceof Path path && s instanceof Path path1)\n for numerics because of type promotion equivalence semantics.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Number> numberComparator = (f, s) -> NumberHelper.compare(f, s);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Vertex, Edge, VertexProperty by id.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Element> elementComparator = Comparator.comparing(Element::id, this);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Property first by key, then by value.\n   */\n element-by-element in the order presented by their natural iterator.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Iterable> iterableComparator = (f, s) -> {\n    final Iterator fi = f.iterator();\n    final fi.hasNext() ? 1 : si.hasNext() ? -1 : 0;\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Compare by sorted elements.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Set> setComparator = (s1, s2) -> {\n    final List l1 = new ArrayList(s1);\n    final List l2 = new ArrayList(s2);\n    Collections.sort(l1, ORDERABILITY);\n    Collections.sort(l2, ORDERABILITY);\n    return iterableComparator.compare(l1, l2);\n  };\n\n ArrayList(m2.entrySet());\n    Collections.sort(l1, ORDERABILITY);\n    Collections.sort(l2, ORDERABILITY);\n    return iterableComparator.compare(l1, l2);\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Map.Entry first by this);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort using either their natural order if they are Comparable or by classname then by toString() if they are\n   * not naturally Comparable. static org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.util.NumberHelper.eitherAreNaN;\n\n/**\n * This implementation extends the default one in Gremlin to avoid throwing exception in case the comparison is between different types or just with a null value.\n * Throwing exceptions in Java is expensive and should be avoided for such cases.\n *\n * @author Luca Garulli (l.garulli@arcadedata.com)\n * @author Mike Personick (http://github.com/mikepersonick)\n */\npublic abstract class GremlinValueComparator implements Comparator<Object> {\n\n  /**\n   * Orderability comparator allows for a total order across all types (no type error exceptions).\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator ORDERABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Orderability semantics.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // nulls first\n      if (f == null || s == null)\n        return f == s ? 0 : f == null ? -1 : 1;\n\n      final Type ft = Type.type(f);\n      /*\n       * Do a quick check to catch very common cases - class equality or both numerics. Even if these are false we\n       * could still be dealing with the same type (e.g. different implementation of List), but let the type\n       * lookup method handle that.\n       */\n      final Type st = f.getClass().equals(s.getClass()) || (f instanceof Number && s instanceof Number) ? ft : Type.type(s);\n\n      return ft != st ? ft.priority() - st.priority() : comparator(ft).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test for equivalence (Orderability semantics).\n     */\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      return compare(f, s) == 0;\n    }\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Comparability has very similar semantics to orderability with the following exceptions:\n   * <p>\n   * 1. NaN is not equal to anything, including itself, and cannot be compared to anything:\n   * equals(NaN, anything) = FALSE\n   * compare(NaN, anything) = ERROR\n   * 2. Unlike Orderability, Comparability is limited to a single type space:\n   * compare(type1, type2) = ERROR\n   * <p>\n   * Note that because of type errors for Comparability, equals(a,b) does not necessarily produce the same result\n   * as compare(a,b) == 0. Make sure to use equals(a,b) for P.eq/neq.\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator COMPARABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Comparability semantics. Throws type errors for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.lt/lte/gt/gte.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n      final Type type = Type.type(f);\n      return comparator(type).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test two Gremlin values for equality per the Comparability semantics. Returns false for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.eq/neq.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // shortcut a long, drawn out element by element comparison\n      if (containersOfDifferentSize(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      try {\n        // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n        final Type type = Type.type(f);\n        return comparator(type).compare(f, s) == 0;\n      } catch (IllegalStateException ex) {\n        /**\n         * By routing through the compare(f, s) path we expose ourselves to type errors, which should be\n         * reduced to false for equality:\n         *\n         * compare(NaN, anything) -> ERROR -> FALSE for equality\n         * compare(Type1, Type2) -> ERROR -> FALSE for equality\n         *\n         * Can also happen for elements nested inside of collections.\n         */\n        return false;\n      }\n    }\n\n    private boolean containersOfDifferentSize(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      if (f instanceof Collection collection && s instanceof Collection collection1)\n        if (collection.size() != (collection1.size()))\n          return true;\n\n      if (f instanceof Path path && s instanceof Path path1)\n        if (path.size() != (path1.size()))\n          return true;\n\n      return false;\n    }\n  };\n\n  private static <T> T throwTypeError() {\n    throw new GremlinTypeErrorException();\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Boolean, Date, String, UUID.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Comparable> naturalOrderComparator = Comparator.naturalOrder();\n\n  /**\n   * This comparator does not provide a stable order for numerics because of type promotion equivalence semantics.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Number> numberComparator = (f, s) -> NumberHelper.compare(f, s);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Vertex, Edge, VertexProperty by id.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Element> elementComparator = Comparator.comparing(Element::id, this);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Property first by key, then by value.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Property> propertyComparator = Comparator.<Property, Object>comparing(Property::key, this).thenComparing(Property::value, this);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort List, Set, Path, and Map element-by-element in the order presented by their natural iterator.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Iterable> iterableComparator = (f, s) -> {\n    final Iterator fi = f.iterator();\n    final Iterator si = s.iterator();\n\n    while (fi.hasNext() && si.hasNext()) {\n      final int i = this.compare(fi.next(), si.next());\n      if (i != 0) {\n        return i;\n      }\n    }\n\n    return fi.hasNext() ? 1 : si.hasNext() ? -1 : 0;\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Compare by sorted elements.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Set> setComparator = (s1, s2) -> {\n    final List l1 = new ArrayList(s1);\n    final List l2 = new ArrayList(s2);\n    Collections.sort(l1, ORDERABILITY);\n    Collections.sort(l2, ORDERABILITY);\n    return iterableComparator.compare(l1, l2);\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Compare by sorted entry-set.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Map> mapComparator = (m1, m2) -> {\n    final List l1 = new ArrayList(m1.entrySet());\n    final List l2 = new ArrayList(m2.entrySet());\n    Collections.sort(l1, ORDERABILITY);\n    Collections.sort(l2, ORDERABILITY);\n    return iterableComparator.compare(l1, l2);\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Sort Map.Entry first by key, then by value.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Map.Entry> entryComparator = Comparator.<Map.Entry, Object>comparing(Map.Entry::getKey, this)\n      .thenComparing(Map.Entry::getValue, this);\n\n  /**\n   * Sort using either their natural order if they are Comparable or by classname then by toString() if they are\n   * not naturally Comparable. Also handles the special case: f.equals(s) -> 0 even for objects without a natural\n   * comparator.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Object> unknownTypeComparator = (f, s) -> naturallyComparable(f, s) ?\n      naturallyCompare(f, s) :\n      Comparator.comparing(o -> o.getClass().getName()).thenComparing(Object::toString).compare(f, s);\n\n  /**\n   * Two nulls. Always 0.\n   */\n  private final Comparator<Object> nulltypeComparator = (f, s) -> 0;\n\n  /**\n   * The typespace. The ordinal of the type indicates its position in cross-type ordering.\n   */\n  public enum Type {\n    Nulltype, Boolean(Boolean.class), Number(Number.class), Date(Date.class), String(String.class), UUID(UUID.class), Vertex(Vertex.class), Edge(\n        Edge.class), VertexProperty(VertexProperty.class), Property(Property.class), Path(Path.class), Set(Set.class), List(List.class), Map(\n        Map.class), MapEntry(Map.Entry.class), Unknown(Object.class);\n\n    /**\n     * Lookup by instanceof semantics (not class equality). Current implementation will return first enum value\n     * that matches the object's type.\n     */\n    public static Type type(final Object o) {\n      if (o == null)\n        return Nulltype;\n\n      final Type[] types = Type.values();\n      for (int i = 1; i < types.length; i++) {\n        if (types[i].type.isInstance(o)) {\n          return types[i];\n        }\n      }\n      return Unknown;\n    }\n\n    Type() {\n      this.type = null;\n    }\n\n    Type(final Class type) {\n      this.type = type;\n    }\n\n    private final Class type;\n\n    public int priority() {\n      return ordinal();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Compare the two objects using their natural comparator. Also handles the special case: f.equals(s) -> 0 even\n   * for objects without a natural comparator.\n   */\n  private static int naturallyCompare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n    if (f instanceof Comparable comparable && s instanceof Comparable)\n      return comparable.compareTo(s);\n    return f.equals(s) ? 0 : throwTypeError();\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Return true if the two objects in the UNKNOWN type space are equal or comparable via their own natural Comparator.\n   */\n  private static boolean naturallyComparable(final Object f, final Object s) {\n    return (f instanceof Comparable && s instanceof Comparable && (f.getClass().isInstance(s) || s.getClass().isInstance(f))) || f.equals(s);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Return true if the two objects are of the same comparison type (although they may not be the exact same Class)\n   */\n  private static boolean comparable(final Object f, final Object s) {\n    if (f == null || s == null)\n      return f == s; // true iff both in the null space\n\n    final Type ft = Type.type(f);\n    final Type st = Type.type(s);\n\n    // Check for same type. If they're both the unknown type then return true iff they are naturally Comparable\n    return ft == Type.Unknown && st == Type.Unknown ? naturallyComparable(f, s) : ft == st;\n  }\n\n  private final Map<Type, Comparator> comparators = new EnumMap<Type, Comparator>(Type.class) {{\n    put(Type.Nulltype, nulltypeComparator);\n    put(Type.Boolean, naturalOrderComparator);\n    put(Type.Number, numberComparator);\n    put(Type.Date, naturalOrderComparator);\n    put(Type.String, naturalOrderComparator);\n    put(Type.UUID, naturalOrderComparator);\n    put(Type.Vertex, elementComparator);\n    put(Type.Edge, elementComparator);\n    put(Type.VertexProperty, elementComparator);\n    put(Type.Property, propertyComparator);\n    put(Type.Path, iterableComparator);\n    put(Type.Set, setComparator);\n    put(Type.List, iterableComparator);\n    put(Type.Map, mapComparator);\n    put(Type.MapEntry, entryComparator);\n    put(Type.Unknown, unknownTypeComparator);\n  }};\n\n  private GremlinValueComparator() {\n  }\n\n  protected Comparator comparator(final Type type) {\n    return comparators.get(type);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Compare(a,b) is defined differently for Comparablity vs. Orderability.\n   */\n  public abstract int compare(final Object f, final Object s);\n\n  /**\n   * Equals(a,b) is defined differently for Comparablity (equality) vs. Orderability (equivalence).\n   */\n  public abstract boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s);\n\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":158,"oldLines":7,"newStart":158,"newLines":7,"lines":["   };"," ","   private static <T> T throwTypeError() {","-    throw new GremlinTypeErrorException();","+    throw new IllegalStateException(\"Objects are not comparable\");","   }"," ","   /**"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.process.traversal.Path;\nimport org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.structure.Edge;\nimport org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.structure.Element;\nimport org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.structure.Property;\nimport org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.structure.Vertex;\nimport org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.structure.VertexProperty;\n\nimport java.util.*;\n\nimport static org.apache.tinkerpop.gremlin.util.NumberHelper.eitherAreNaN;\n\n/**\n * This implementation extends the default one in Gremlin to avoid throwing exception in case the comparison is between different types or just with a null value.\n * Throwing exceptions in Java is expensive and should be avoided for such cases.\n *\n * @author Luca Garulli (l.garulli@arcadedata.com)\n * @author Mike Personick (http://github.com/mikepersonick)\n */\npublic abstract class GremlinValueComparator implements Comparator<Object> {\n\n  /**\n   * Orderability comparator allows for a total order across all types (no type error exceptions).\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator ORDERABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Orderability semantics.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // nulls first\n      if (f == null || s == null)\n        return f == s ? 0 : f == null ? -1 : 1;\n\n      final Type ft = Type.type(f);\n      /*\n       * Do a quick check to catch very common cases - class equality or both numerics. Even if these are false we\n       * could still be dealing with the same type (e.g. different implementation of List), but let the type\n       * lookup method handle that.\n       */\n      final Type st = f.getClass().equals(s.getClass()) || (f instanceof Number && s instanceof Number) ? ft : Type.type(s);\n\n      return ft != st ? ft.priority() - st.priority() : comparator(ft).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test for equivalence (Orderability semantics).\n     */\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      return compare(f, s) == 0;\n    }\n  };\n\n  /**\n   * Comparability has very similar semantics to orderability with the following exceptions:\n   * <p>\n   * 1. NaN is not equal to anything, including itself, and cannot be compared to anything:\n   * equals(NaN, anything) = FALSE\n   * compare(NaN, anything) = ERROR\n   * 2. Unlike Orderability, Comparability is limited to a single type space:\n   * compare(type1, type2) = ERROR\n   * <p>\n   * Note that because of type errors for Comparability, equals(a,b) does not necessarily produce the same result\n   * as compare(a,b) == 0. Make sure to use equals(a,b) for P.eq/neq.\n   */\n  public static final GremlinValueComparator COMPARABILITY = new GremlinValueComparator() {\n\n    /**\n     * Compare two Gremlin value objects per the Comparability semantics. Throws type errors for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.lt/lte/gt/gte.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public int compare(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        throwTypeError();\n\n      // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n      final Type type = Type.type(f);\n      return comparator(type).compare(f, s);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test two Gremlin values for equality per the Comparability semantics. Returns false for NaN comparison\n     * and for cross-type comparison (including nulltype).\n     *\n     * Use this method for P.eq/neq.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      // shortcut a long, drawn out element by element comparison\n      if (containersOfDifferentSize(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare, NaN always produces ERROR\n      if (eitherAreNaN(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      // For Compare we do not cross type boundaries, including null\n      if (!comparable(f, s))\n        return false;\n\n      try {\n        // comparable(f, s) assures that type(f) == type(s)\n        final Type type = Type.type(f);\n        return comparator(type).compare(f, s) == 0;\n      } catch (IllegalStateException ex) {\n        /**\n         * By routing through the compare(f, s) path we expose ourselves to type errors, which should be\n         * reduced to false for equality:\n         *\n         * compare(NaN, anything) -> ERROR -> FALSE for equality\n         * compare(Type1, Type2) -> ERROR -> FALSE for equality\n         *\n         * Can also happen for elements nested inside of collections.\n         */\n        return false;\n      }\n    }\n\n    private boolean containersOfDifferentSize(final Object f, final Object s) {\n      if (f instanceof Collection collection && s instanceof Collection collection1)\n        if (collection.size() != (collection1.size()))\n          return true;\n\n      if (f instanceof Path path && s instanceof Path path1)\n        if Transform a cypher expression into Gremlin.\n *\n * @author Luca Garulli (l.garulli@arcadedata.com)\n */\n\npublic class ArcadeCypher extends ArcadeGremlin {\n  private static final Map<String, private              String                       cypher;\n\n  private static class CachedStatement {\n    public final String cypher;\n    public final String gremlin;\n    public       int    used = 0;\n\n    private CachedStatement(final String cypher, final String gremlin) {\n      this.cypher = cypher;\n      this.gremlin = gremlin;\n    }\n  }\n\n  public ArcadeCypher(final ArcadeGraph graph, final String cypherQuery) {\n    super(graph, null);\n    this.cypher = cypherQuery;\n  }\n\n  @Override\n  public ResultSet execute() {\n    if (query == null) {\n      try {\n        compileToGremlin(graph, parameters);\n      } catch (final SyntaxException e) {\n        throw new CommandParsingException(e);\n      }\n\n    }\n    final ResultSet resultSet = super.execute();\n    final InternalResultSet result = new InternalResultSet() {\n      @Override\n      public Optional<ExecutionPlan> getExecutionPlan() {\n        return resultSet.getExecutionPlan();\n      }\n    };\n\n    while (resultSet.hasNext()) {\n      final Result next = resultSet.next();\n      if (next.isElement())\n        result.add(next);\n      else {\n        // unpack single result projections\n        final Map<String, Object> map = next.toMap();\n        final Object nextValue = map.values().iterator().next();\n        if (map.size() == 1 && nextValue instanceof Map<?, ?> map1) {\n          result.addAll(CypherQueryEngine.transformMap(map1));\n        } else {\n          result.addAll(CypherQueryEngine.transformMap(map));\n        }\n      }\n    }\n    return result;\n  }\n\n  @Override\n  public QueryEngine.AnalyzedQuery parse() {\n    if (query == null) {\n      try {\n        compileToGremlin(graph, parameters);\n      } catch (final SyntaxException e) {\n        throw new CommandParsingException(e);\n      }\n    }\n\n    return super.parse();\n  }\n\n  record CypherQueryAndParameters(String cypherQuery, Map<String, Object> parameters) {\n  }\n\n  public void compileToGremlin(final ArcadeGraph graph, final Map<String, Object> parameters) {\n\n    CypherQueryAndParameters queryAndParameters = replaceParameterNames(cypher, Optional.ofNullable(parameters).orElse(Map.of()));\n\n    // Expand ALL(k IN keys($param) WHERE ...) patterns before translation\n    queryAndParameters = expandAllKeysPattern(queryAndParameters);\n\n    this.parameters = queryAndParameters.parameters;\n    if (CACHE_SIZE == 0)\n      // NO CACHE\n      query = parameters != null ?\n          new TranslationFacade().toGremlinGroovy(queryAndParameters.cypherQuery, queryAndParameters.parameters) :\n          new TranslationFacade().toGremlinGroovy(queryAndParameters.cypherQuery);\n\n    final String db = graph.getDatabase().getDatabasePath();\n\n    final String mapKey = db + \":\" + queryAndParameters.cypherQuery;\n\n    final CachedStatement cached = STATEMENT_CACHE.get(mapKey);\n    // FOUND\n    if (cached != null) {\n      ++cached.used;\n      query = cached.gremlin;\n    }\n\n    // TRANSLATE TO GREMLIN AND CACHE THE STATEMENT FOR FURTHER USAGE\n    final CypherAst ast = parameters == null ?\n        CypherAst.parse(queryAndParameters.cypherQuery) :\n        CypherAst.parse(queryAndParameters.cypherQuery, queryAndParameters.parameters);\n    final Translator<String, GroovyPredicate> translator = Translator.builder().gremlinGroovy().enableCypherExtensions().build();\n    String gremlin = ast.buildTranslation(translator);\n\n    // REPLACE '  cypher.null' WITH NULL\n    gremlin = gremlin.replace(\"'  cypher.null'\", \"null\");\n\n    cacheLastStatement(cypher, gremlin);\n\n    query = gremlin;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Expands ALL(k IN keys($param) WHERE var[k] = $param[k]) patterns into explicit property comparisons.\n   * This works around opencypher-gremlin's lack of support for this pattern.\n   * <p>\n   * Example: ALL(k IN keys($props) WHERE n[k] = $props[k]) with $props={age:30, city:\"NYC\"}\n   * becomes: n.age = $props_age AND n.city = $props_city\n   */\n  private static CypherQueryAndParameters expandAllKeysPattern(CypherQueryAndParameters queryAndParams) {\n    String query = queryAndParams.cypherQuery;\n    Map<String, Object> parameters = new HashMap<>(queryAndParams.parameters);\n\n    // Pattern: ALL(variableName IN keys($paramName) WHERE nodeVar[variableName] = $paramName[variableName])\n    // We need to match this pattern and expand it\n    java.util.regex.Pattern pattern = java.util.regex.Pattern.compile(\n        \"ALL\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*(\\\\w+)\\\\s+IN\\\\s+keys\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*\\\\$([\\\\w]+)\\\\s*\\\\)\\\\s+WHERE\\\\s+(\\\\w+)\\\\s*\\\\[\\\\s*\\\\1\\\\s*\\\\]\\\\s*=\\\\s*\\\\$\\\\2\\\\s*\\\\[\\\\s*\\\\1\\\\s*\\\\]\\\\s*\\\\)\",\n        java.util.regex.Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE\n    );\n\n    java.util.regex.Matcher matcher = pattern.matcher(query);\n    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder();\n    int lastEnd = 0;\n\n    while (matcher.find()) {\n      String iterVar = matcher.group(1);        // e.g., \"k\"\n      String paramName = matcher.group(2);      // e.g., \"props\" (the randomized parameter name)\n      String nodeVar = matcher.group(3);        // e.g., \"n\"\n\n      // Get the parameter value (should be a Map)\n      Object paramValue = parameters.get(paramName);\n\n      if (paramValue instanceof Map<?, ?> paramMap) {\n        // Build the expanded condition\n        StringBuilder expanded = new StringBuilder();\n        boolean first = true;\n\n        for (Map.Entry<?, ?> entry : paramMap.entrySet()) {\n          String key = entry.getKey().toString();\n          Object value = entry.getValue();\n\n          if (!first) {\n            expanded.append(\" AND \");\n          }\n          first = false;\n\n          // Create a new parameter for this specific key\n          String newParamName = paramName + \"_\" + key;\n          expanded.append(nodeVar).append(\".\").append(key).append(\" = $\").append(newParamName);\n          parameters.put(newParamName, value);\n        }\n\n        // If the map is empty, this should always be true (vacuous truth)\n        if (paramMap.isEmpty()) {\n          expanded.append(\"true\");\n        }\n\n        // Replace the ALL pattern with the expanded condition\n        result.append(query, lastEnd, matcher.start());\n        result.append(\"(\").append(expanded).append(\")\");\n        lastEnd = matcher.end();\n      } else {\n        // If parameter is not a map, leave the pattern as is (will likely fail during translation)\n        result.append(query, lastEnd, matcher.end());\n        lastEnd = matcher.end();\n      }\n    }\n\n    result.append(query.substring(lastEnd));\n    return new CypherQueryAndParameters(result.toString(), parameters);\n  }\n\n  public static CypherQueryAndParameters replaceParameterNames(String cypher, Map<String, Object> parameters) {\n    Map<String, String> replacementMap = new HashMap<>();\n    String newCypher = cypher;\n\n    for (String oldKey : parameters.keySet()) {\n      // Generate a random 4-character string\n      String newKey;\n      do {\n        newKey = generateRandomString(4);\n      } while (replacementMap.containsValue(newKey)); // Ensure uniqueness\n\n      // Replace occurrences of the old key in the cypher cypherQuery\n      String placeholder = \"\\\\$\" + oldKey; // Escape the $ symbol for regex\n      newCypher = newCypher.replaceAll(placeholder, \"\\\\$\" + newKey);\n\n      // Add the new key to the replacement map\n      replacementMap.put(oldKey, newKey);\n    }\n\n    // Update the parameters map with the new keys\n    Map<String, Object> updatedParameters = new HashMap<>();\n    for (Map.Entry<String, Object> entry : parameters.entrySet()) {\n      updatedParameters.put(replacementMap.get(entry.getKey()), entry.getValue());\n    }\n\n    return new CypherQueryAndParameters(newCypher, updatedParameters);\n  }\n\n  private static String generateRandomString(int length) {\n    String chars = \"abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz\";\n    StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(length);\n    ThreadLocalRandom random = ThreadLocalRandom.current();\n    for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n      sb.append(chars.charAt(random.nextInt(chars.length())));\n    }\n    return sb.toString();\n  }\n\n  public static void closeDatabase(final ArcadeGraph graph) {\n    final String mapKey = graph.getDatabase().getDatabasePath() + \":\";\n\n    // REMOVE ALL THE ENTRIES RELATIVE TO THE CLOSED DATABASE\n    STATEMENT_CACHE.entrySet().removeIf(stringCachedStatementEntry -> stringCachedStatementEntry.getKey().startsWith(mapKey));\n  }\n\n  private static void cacheLastStatement(String cypher, String gremlin) {\n    if (CACHE_SIZE > 0) {\n      // REMOVE THE OLDEST TO MAKE ROOM FOR THE LATEST STATEMENT\n      while (STATEMENT_CACHE.size() >= CACHE_SIZE) {\n        int leastUsedValue = 0;\n        String leastUsedKey = null;\n\n        for (final Map.Entry<String, CachedStatement> entry : STATEMENT_CACHE.entrySet()) {\n          if (leastUsedKey == null || entry.getValue().used < leastUsedValue) {\n            leastUsedKey = entry.getKey();\n            leastUsedValue = entry.getValue().used;\n          }\n        }\n        STATEMENT_CACHE.remove(leastUsedKey);\n      }\n      STATEMENT_CACHE.put(cypher, new CachedStatement(cypher, gremlin));\n    }\n  }\n}\n","numLines":296,"startLine":1,"totalLines":296}}}

public void compileToGremlin(final ArcadeGraph graph, final Map<String, Object> parameters) {\n\n    CypherQueryAndParameters queryAndParameters = replaceParameterNames(cypher, Optional.ofNullable(parameters).orElse(Map.of()));\n\n    // Expand ALL(k IN keys($param) WHERE ...) patterns before translation\n    queryAndParameters = expandAllKeysPattern(queryAndParameters);\n\n $param[k]) patterns into explicit property comparisons.\n   * This works around opencypher-gremlin's lack of support for this pattern.\n   * <p>\n   * Example: ALL(k IN keys($props) WHERE n[k] = $props[k]) with $props={age:30, city:\"NYC\"}\n   * becomes: n.age = $props_age AND n.city = $props_city\n   */\n  private static CypherQueryAndParameters expandAllKeysPattern(CypherQueryAndParameters queryAndParams) {\n    String query = queryAndParams.cypherQuery;\n    Map<String, Object> parameters = new HashMap<>(queryAndParams.parameters);\n\n    // Pattern: ALL(variableName IN keys($paramName) WHERE nodeVar[variableName] = $paramName[variableName])\n    // We need to match this pattern and expand it\n    java.util.regex.Pattern pattern = java.util.regex.Pattern.compile(\n        \"ALL\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*(\\\\w+)\\\\s+IN\\\\s+keys\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*\\\\$([\\\\w]+)\\\\s*\\\\)\\\\s+WHERE\\\\s+(\\\\w+)\\\\s*\\\\[\\\\s*\\\\1\\\\s*\\\\]\\\\s*=\\\\s*\\\\$\\\\2\\\\s*\\\\[\\\\s*\\\\1\\\\s*\\\\]\\\\s*\\\\)\",\n        java.util.regex.Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE\n    );\n\n    java.util.regex.Matcher matcher = pattern.matcher(query);\n    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder();\n    int lastEnd = 0;\n\n    while (matcher.find()) {\n      String iterVar = matcher.group(1);        // e.g., \"k\"\n      String paramName = matcher.group(2);      // e.g., \"props\" (the randomized parameter name)\n      String nodeVar = matcher.group(3);        // e.g., \"n\"\n\n      // Get the parameter value (should be a Map)\n      Object paramValue = parameters.get(paramName);\n\n      if (paramValue instanceof Map<?, ?> paramMap) {\n        // Build the expanded condition\n        StringBuilder expanded = new StringBuilder();\n        boolean first = true;\n\n        for (Map.Entry<?, ?> entry : paramMap.entrySet()) {\n          String key = entry.getKey().toString();\n          Object value = entry.getValue();\n\n          if (!first) {\n            expanded.append(\" AND \");\n          }\n          first = false;\n\n          // Create a new parameter for this specific key\n          String newParamName = paramName + \"_\" + key;\n          expanded.append(nodeVar).append(\".\").append(key).append(\" = $\").append(newParamName);\n          parameters.put(newParamName, value);\n        }\n\n        // If the map is empty, this should always be true (vacuous truth)\n        if (paramMap.isEmpty()) {\n          expanded.append(\"true\");\n        }\n\n        // Replace the ALL pattern with the expanded condition\n        result.append(query, lastEnd, matcher.start());\n        result.append(\"(\").append(expanded).append(\")\");\n        lastEnd = matcher.end();\n      } else {\n        // If parameter is not a map, leave the pattern as is (will likely fail during translation)\n        result.append(query, lastEnd, matcher.end());\n        lastEnd = matcher.end();\n      }\n    }\n\n    result.append(query.substring(lastEnd));\n    return new CypherQueryAndParameters(result.toString(), parameters);\n  }\n\n  public static CypherQueryAndParameters replaceParameterNames(String cypher, Map<String, Object> parameters) {\n    Map<String, String> replacementMap = new HashMap<>();\n    String newCypher = cypher;\n\n    for (String oldKey : parameters.keySet()) {\n      // Generate a random 4-character string\n      String newKey;\n      do {\n        newKey = generateRandomString(4);\n      } while (replacementMap.containsValue(newKey)); // Ensure uniqueness\n\n      // Replace occurrences of the old key in the cypher cypherQuery\n      String placeholder = \"\\\\$\" + oldKey; // Escape the $ symbol for regex\n      newCypher = newCypher.replaceAll(placeholder, \"\\\\$\" + newKey);\n\n      // Add the new key to the replacement map\n      replacementMap.put(oldKey, newKey);\n    }\n\n    // Update the parameters map with the new keys\n    Map<String, Object> updatedParameters = new HashMap<>();\n    for (Map.Entry<String, Object> entry : parameters.entrySet()) {\n      updatedParameters.put(replacementMap.get(entry.getKey()), entry.getValue());\n    }\n\n    return new CypherQueryAndParameters(newCypher, updatedParameters);\n  }\n\n  private static String generateRandomString(int length) {\n    String chars = \"abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz\";\n    StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(length);\n    ThreadLocalRandom random = ThreadLocalRandom.current();\n    for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n      sb.append(chars.charAt(random.nextInt(chars.length())));\n    }\n    return sb.toString();\n  }\n\n  public static void closeDatabase(final ArcadeGraph graph) {\n    final String mapKey = graph.getDatabase().getDatabasePath() + \":\";\n\n    // REMOVE ALL THE ENTRIES RELATIVE TO THE CLOSED DATABASE\n    STATEMENT_CACHE.entrySet().removeIf(stringCachedStatementEntry -> stringCachedStatementEntry.getKey().startsWith(mapKey));\n  }\n\n  private static void cacheLastStatement(String cypher, String gremlin) {\n    if (CACHE_SIZE > 0) {\n      // REMOVE THE OLDEST TO MAKE ROOM FOR THE LATEST STATEMENT\n      while (STATEMENT_CACHE.size() >= CACHE_SIZE) {\n        int leastUsedValue = 0;\n        String leastUsedKey = null;\n\n        for (final Map.Entry<String, CachedStatement> entry : STATEMENT_CACHE.entrySet()) {\n          if (leastUsedKey == null || entry.getValue().used < leastUsedValue) {\n            leastUsedKey = entry.getKey();\n            leastUsedValue = entry.getValue().used;\n          }\n        }\n        STATEMENT_CACHE.remove(leastUsedKey);\n      }\n      STATEMENT_CACHE.put(cypher, new CachedStatement(cypher, gremlin));\n    }\n  }\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":148,"oldLines":7,"newStart":148,"newLines":11,"lines":["     final CypherAst ast = parameters == null ?","
